台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

聯強(2347)：資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢
不變 
建議：維持買進

一、聯強為亞太地區最大 3C 通
路商，營收成長以半導體業務最
佳  
 聯強為 3C 電子產品之銷售、配送、維修、量身訂做即時生產之專業通路商，為
亞太地區 3C 通路商龍頭，2026 年前三季產品比重分別為商用加值佔 31%、資
訊消費佔 17% 、 半 導 體 佔 48% 、 手 機 產 品 佔 4% ，成長主要來商用加值
(+112%YoY)和半導體業務(+162%YoY)。聯強海外市場包含中國、香港、紐澳、
泰國、印尼、印度及越南等地，其中以中國、台灣和印尼等區域較佳，營收分別
為 YoY+109%、YoY+78%和 YoY+18%。聯強競爭優勢為透過其四合一經營模
式，搭配其綿密的通路發揮規模經濟，並提供高附加價值的後勤運籌(庫存管理
與快速維為修服務等)，於中國主要競爭對手為神州數碼、天音通信控股。

二、半導體業務成長性佳，3Q26 
EPS 2.93 元創新高並優於預期 
 3Q26 營收 2366 億元(+18%QoQ；+136%YoY)優於預估值 14%，其中商用加
值和半導體營收分別為+116%YOY 和+273%YoY，因秉棄部分利潤不佳專案，
毛利率 3.49%優於前季之 3.4%，營業利益 61.41 億元(+28%%QoQ)優於預估
值 24%，因營運資金需求以及籌資成本上升致使利息費用提高至 9.34 億元
(+106%QoQ)，稅後純益 48.83 億元(+25%QoQ；+90%YoY)，EPS 2.93 元優
於預期之 2.5 元並創新高。

三、資金成本上揚導致業務拓展
較為謹慎，估計 4Q26 EPS 2.34
元 
 由於資金成本上揚致使公司放棄部分利潤不佳半導體業務，十月營收月減 34%，
估計 4Q26 營收 2046 億元(-14%QoQ；YoY+62%)，因產品組合變化轉佳，毛
利率提升至 3.58%，稅後純益 38.99 億元(-20%QoQ；+41%YoY)，EPS 2.34
元。

四、AI 長線需求有助商用加值和
半導體業務成長性 
 集邦 預估 2026 和 2027 年全 球九 大 CSP 資 本支 出分 別為 0.95 兆 美元
(+106%YoY)和 1.53 兆美元(+60%YoY)，且 AI 需求從雲端擴展到企業端，其中
以商用加值和半導體業務受惠幅度最大。 
在商用加值業務方面，在台灣和中國資料中心產品出貨挹注下，3Q26 營收
+116%YoY。展望 2027 年，中國十十五規劃和國產化政策推動政府和國企加速
採用本土品牌，推升聯強代理華為、浪潮等品牌業務成長性，台灣則是接獲數個
半導體大廠 AI 基礎建設專案，加計中小企業 AI 建置需求逐步顯現，商用業務將
為營運成長主要動能之一。 
在半導體業務方面，缺貨和漲價效致使記憶體、SSD 和硬碟業務為半導體業務
中 成 長 性 最 佳 者 ， 且 AI 推 論 亦 增 加 CPU 需求，3Q26 半 導 體 業 務 營 收

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報 
YoY+273%，半導體業務為營運成長另一重點業務，但因資金成本上揚，公司將
摒棄部分利潤未達目標專案，此將影響短期營收動能。 
受惠於出貨 ASP 增加，以及原廠擴大促銷力道挽救銷售量，3Q26 資訊家用和
手機業務分別+21%YoY 和+33%YoY。記憶體大漲致使原廠逐步反應成本於 PC
和手機新機售價，並將導致消費者延長換機週期，2027 年此兩塊業務成長性仍
相對保守看待。

五、資金成本上揚將致使接案更
為謹慎，但長期營益率好轉方向
不變 
 聯強今年接獲數個大型 AI 基礎建設專案，致使營收動能以及營運資金需求大增，
3Q26 遇到資金成本上揚，致使利息費用較前季倍增，公司將透過聯貸、可轉債
以及現增降低利息負擔，預期三項籌資工具皆完成後可望降低利息費用 20-
30%。展望 2027 年，儘管營收成長性不如 2026 年亮眼，但接單策略轉趨謹慎
將致使毛利率下滑壓力降低，且預期營業費用增幅有限下，營益率持續好轉方向
不變。

六、維持買進理由  1)預估 2026 和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 9.38 元和 11.07 元。 
2)儘管資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢不變，維持買進
評等，TP 100 元 (2027 PE x 9)。

### Page 1

3Q26 GM slightly lower: Off revenue of NT$120,744mn (+26% QoQ/+66% YoY),
GM contracted by 2.1ppt QoQ to 17.6% (vs. MSe and consensus both at 18.5%) on
higher component costs. With opex of NT$5,162mn (opex ratio 4.3% vs. MSe 4.3%/
consensus  4.0%), OP was NT$16,122mn (OPM 13.4%), 6%/5% lower than MSe of NT
$17,183mn (OPM 14.2%)/consensus  of NT$17,047mn (OPM 14.4%), respectively. With
a narrower non-op gain of NT$94mn, net profit came in at NT$13,074mn (EPS NT

$23.4), 2%/5% short of MSe/consensus of NT$13,328mn (EPS NT$23.9)/NT
$13,748mn (EPS NT$24.5).
Outlook still on optimistic side: We expect Accton to grow revenue sequentially
into 4Q26. Network switch customers will likely continue to pull in more for their AI
clusters amid 800G migration. Meanwhile, its AI accelerator customer should ramp
more of its new generation product and increase adoption of liquid cooling thermal
solutions. To mitigate pressure from elevated component costs, it has negotiated

with customers for them to consign some components, e.g., DRAM, from late 3Q26.
Although this might cap revenue growth upside for Accton, it should help on the
GM side. We model revenue of NT$130,083mn (+8% QoQ/+81% YoY), gross margin
of 20.0% (+2.4ppt QoQ), operating margin of 15.5% (+2.1ppt QoQ)  and EPS of NT
$28.2 (+21% QoQ/+88% YoY). Looking to 2027, we see revenue growth of NT
$526,019mn (+26% YoY) and GM of 20.0%, for EPS NT$112.9 EPS (+31% YoY).

Stay OW: We believe Accton should continue to benefit from networking and
compute demand growth amid AI deployment, reflecting its system integration
capability, customer relationships, and execution. For the longer term,  it will likely
tap into opportunities in rack level solutions, optical-based switches, etc. We lower
our PT to NT$2,250 (20x 2027 P/E) from NT$3,500 (30x 2027 P/E), mostly owing
to more conservative sentiment toward AI names.
What’s Changed

Accton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 GM Fell Short Slightly;
Better Margin Trend Ahead
Although 3Q26 earnings came up short on lower GM, we expect
Accton to deliver high single digit % revenue growth with GM
back to ~20% in 4Q26, reflecting some component consignment
arrangements.  Margin expansion in 4Q26 should be a positive
catalyst for the share price in the coming months.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
OW with PT of NT$2,250 (previously NT$3,500)
Price target (base case scenario value) –  NT$2,250 (-36%): Our new price target is based
on 20x our 2027 EPS estimate (30x previously). We lower our target multiple to factor in
recent market sentiment toward AI names. We see our target multiple supported by the
45% operating profit CAGR we project for 2025-28. This implies a PEG of 0.4 vs. its
average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for an operating

profit CAGR of 32%.
Bull case scenario value – NT$3,350 (-20%): We apply 30x to our base case EPS estimate
for 2027 (36x previously). This scenario reflects stronger demand for network switches in
both data center and enterprise applications as well as more orders for AI accelerator
modules.
Bear case scenario value – NT$1,800 (-39%): We apply 10x to our base case EPS estimate
for 2027 (16x previously). This scenario reflects lukewarm data center and enterprise

capex and continued dominance by merchant GPUs in AI servers.
Downside risks to our positive thesis
• Weaker data center capex
• Slower network switch spec migration
• InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
• Softer custom AI accelerator growth
• Softer ODM direct business model adoptions

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on preliminary JMTBA orders, which we
believe could be a catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
According to the Japan Machine Tool Builders' Association (JMTBA), headline

orders dropped 8% MoM but grew 43% YoY in October  2026 (vs. +13% MoM/
+60% YoY in September 2026), to ¥204bn.
• Domestic orders were -18% MoM/+47% YoY (vs. +24% MoM/+46% YoY in
September), at ¥52bn.
• Overseas orders were -5% MoM/+41% YoY (vs. +9% MoM/+67% YoY  in
September), at ¥152bn.
Our view:
• For overseas orders, the growth remains solid on a YoY basis. We think the
sequential decline is likely from China as its manufacturing PMI edged down

to 49.3 in Oct (vs. 50.1 in Sep). Meanwhile, North America and Europe could
prove more resilient, with manufacturing PMIs staying relatively firm at
55.5/52.7 in Oct (vs. 54.5/52.9 in Sep)
• We remain OW on both Airtac (1590.TW) and Hiwin (2049.TW), as we see
company-specific drivers on top of the industry recovery trend:
° Airtac should continue to benefit from its own market share gains.
Valuation also appears undemanding at 15x 2027e P/E (vs. 25x average

since 2020).
° Hiwin should  see margin expansion in the coming quarters, driven by
higher utilization and price hikes. Current valuation of 27x 2027 P/E
implies upside vs. peak cycle valuation at 35-40x P/E.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 25x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). We think our valuation of 25x P/E is reasonable (vs. average of 25x since
2020).
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China
n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation

Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple
of 34x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 50% operating profit

CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup
n Market share gains
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand
n More pricing pressure
n Market share loss
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks

hold, and sell but represent recommended relative weightings (see definitions below). To satisfy regulatory requirements, we correspond Overweight, our most positive stock rating, with a

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Document 149: 261117_daiwa_andes

Source PDF: all report pdf/261117_daiwa_andes.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following on our results Memo for Andes, we widen our 
2026E net loss and maintain our Hold (3) rating. Management aims for a 
profitable 4Q26 to cover YTD losses, yet we are unsure how sustainable its 
bottom-line turnaround will be in the near term, given still uncertain timing 
of royalty conversion despite enriched IP and customer portfolios. That 
said, we remain structurally positive on Andes’s outlook, thanks to its 
leading position in the RISC-V architecture well prepared to capitalise on 
the AI cycle from cloud to edge to physical.

What’s the impact: Forecast changes in a mixed bag. We widen our 
2026E net loss for Andes to factor in its higher-than-expected opex YTD, 
but we keep our 2027E EPS largely unchanged to factor in its frugal opex 
control starting from 3Q26. Though bottom-line turnaround looks still 
bumpy in the near term on its suboptimal operating leverage due to still 
suboptimal scale, as a result of still uncertain timing of royalty revenue 
conversion, we merit management’s opex control to speed up bottom-line 
turnaround. This, together with its enriched RISC-V IP portfolio and 
broadened customer base for market-share gain, should help sustain its 
turnaround from 2H27 when topline expands to improve earnings quality 
and investor confidence.

Patience still for a sustainable turnaround. Despite this year’s business 
hiccups on delayed royalty ramp and opex volatility, we remain positive on 
Andes’s structural outlook, given its leading position in the RISC-V 
architecture offering enriched high-performance/low-power compute IP as 
substitutes to Arm and x86 CPU counterparts to capitalise on the secular AI 
cycle, with a broad coverage of applications from accelerated compute and 
embedded control to automotive. Management’s comments support our 
view, as it expects the RISC-V’s open-source ecosystem to drive innovation 
allowing designs of instruction sets tailored to customers’ specific needs, 
which should help enhance customer stickiness while attracting new 
customers for market-share gains. That said, patience may be still needed 
for Andes’s monetisation to bear fruit for a sustainable turnaround.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we raise our 12M 
TP to TWD240 (from TWD180), still based on DCF methodology (WACC: 
18%, terminal growth: 3%) which we believe is appropriate for a startup IP 
company such as Andes. Given the still bumpy road to any sustainable 
turnaround, we maintain our Hold (3) call with key upside/downside risks of 
any faster RISC-V ecosystem build/further delays of royalty ramp.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 29-48% above the consensus 
forecast likely due to our lower opex assumptions for Andes.

17 November 2026

Near-term turnaround still looks bumpy

➢ 2026E net loss likely to widen on opex volatility but … 
➢ … no harm to structural turnaround from 2H27 
➢ Maintaining our Hold (3) rating with a higher 12M TP of TWD240

Company profile 
Founded in 2005, Andes is an embedded microprocessor intellectual property (IP) provider with 
solutions in high-performance and low-power 32/64-bit processors and associated SoC platforms. Its 
solutions can be adopted for diverse applications such as IoT, 5G, AI, Bluetooth/WiFi devices, gaming 
and automotive. As a founding member of RISC-V International, Andes focuses on developing 
processor IPs based on the RISC-V instruction-set architecture while its mature V3 architecture is the 
main contributor of its royalty revenue.

### Page 5

5

Andes Technology (6533 TT): 17 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 17 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Andes’ Board of Directors consists of seven members, including three independent directors,
representing approximately 43% of the total board composition, exceeding the regulatory
minimum requirement of 20%. The roles of Chairman and President are held by separate
individuals, reflecting a clear division of leadership responsibilities.
Currently, the Board includes one female director among the seven seats. Andes acknowledges
that gender diversity at the board level remains an area for improvement and is actively
committed to strengthening diversity and inclusion as part of its ongoing governance
enhancement efforts.
Capital management 1
As a semiconductor IP provider without physical manufacturing operations, Andes does not
require significant capital expenditure for factories. Instead, its primary capital needs are related
to ongoing investment in research and development to support new IP development and capture
emerging market opportunities. From this perspective, we believe Andes is inclined to retain a
portion of its earnings to fund future IP innovation rather than fully distributing profits to
shareholders.
Andes did not distribute dividends prior to 2019. In 2019, it paid out 72% of its 2018 net profit as
dividends. Subsequently, the dividend payout ratio remained high at approximately 90% during
the period from 2020 to 2022. However, the payout ratio declined sharply to 21% in 2023 based
on 2022 net profit, reflecting a more conservative capital allocation approach.
Due to weakened profitability, Andes did not distribute dividends in 2024 or 2025, as earnings
were insufficient to support shareholder payouts. Management has prioritized financial flexibility
and the preservation of resources to navigate challenging market conditions and sustain long-
term R&D investment.
Related party &
transaction 1
Before July 2021, Andes conducted related party transactions with MediaTek (2454 TT) given
each company's chairman was the same person. MediaTek was Andes' largest customer with a
31% revenue contribution in 2013. Its revenue contribution has gradually declined in past years
to a single digit percentage in 2021. Since Andes' board of directors' election in July 2021, its
chairman and MediaTek's chairman are no longer the same person. Andes currently does not
have any meaningful related party transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
As a semiconductor IP provider without physical products, Andes mainly procures softwares &
services from its suppliers. Andes implements supplier management policies to ensure that there
is no violation of corporate social responsibility policy in the procurement contract with its
suppliers.
E Water & wastewater management 1
Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on-
site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment. It has set up a goal to reduce water
consumption by 2% every year through measures such as installing faucet water savers and
promoting the practice of saving water.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1 Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on- site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment.
E GHG emissions 1
Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on-
site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment. However, the company still applies
measures to reduce power consumption for its operations, such as turning off lights one hour per
day during the lunch break. It has set up a goal to reduce its total electricity consumption by 1-
2% every year.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

What’s Changed
WT Microelectronics Co. Ltd.

(3036.TW) From To
Rating Overweight Equal-weight
Price Target NT$296.00 NT$174.00
WPG Holdings (3702.TW) From To
Rating Overweight Equal-weight
Price Target NT$160.00 NT$79.00
Despite strong 3Q26 results and resilient near-term guidance at
both WT Microelectronics and WPG Holdings, we see visibility
risks into 2028. Memory price normalization and rising USD
funding costs prompt us to trim expectations across Greater
China semi distributors.
Peaking momentum meets rising 2027-28 headwinds: Both WT and WPG posted

record top-lines and profit scale in 3Q26 on AI server buildouts and sharp memory
price inflation; yet, we are wary of several late-cycle pressures. First, on AI
infrastructure, our US team recently shifted ~5 GW of US data center capacity from
2028 into 2029 post-US midterms (link), introducing procurement timing risk.
Second, we caution that memory may still be cyclical after all, and we may now be
late into the cycle; (Old Memory: Peak Pricing Power?; Memory – Key Debate Post

Mid-terms (Shawn Kim)).  Third, elevated memory prices are already weighing on
traditional consumer end markets. Finally, rapid revenue expansion has driven heavy
working capital outflows and debt accumulation just as our US Economics team
forecasts two 25 bps Fed hikes in Dec and Mar to 4.25%-4.50%, keeping USD
financing costs elevated (link).
WT Microelectronics (3036.TW) 3Q26 review (link): For 4Q26, management
guided revenue to reaccelerate to a midpoint of NT$750bn (+26% Q/Q, +119% Y/Y)

on high double-digit Q/Q data center growth, with GM stepping down to a midpoint
of 3.24% (-0.36ppt Q/Q) on mix. Net profit guidance was a midpoint of NT$11.5bn
(+12% Q/Q, +17% Y/Y). 4Q26 financing costs were guided up to NT$2.7bn (vs. NT
$1.94bn in 3Q26) with no timeline for operating cash flow turning positive.
WPG Holdings (3702.TW) 3Q26 review: 3Q26 revenue was NT$510bn (+11.8% Q/Q,
+108.6% Y/Y), GM was 4.42%, and net profit was NT$10.39bn (+20.5% Q/Q,

+226.9% Y/Y). Memory surged to 58% of 3Q26 revenue (from ~25% historically)
and compute (AI and server) reached 52%, while communication/mobile mix was 11%
and consumer mix was 9%. Management noted its ship-and-debit model mitigates
inventory write-down risk, and its LaaS platform grew GMV 75.6% Y/Y (revenue
+15.5% Y/Y). For 4Q26, WPG guided revenue at a midpoint of NT$505bn, -1.0% Q/Q,
GM at a midpoint of 4.40%, and EPS at a midpoint of NT$5.47. Financial cost/sales

as at ~0.8% (two-thirds USD-denominated).
Stock implications: WT: downgrading to EW, PT cut to NT$174. 2026/27/28 EPS
revised by +1%/-6%/-5%. WPG: downgrading to EW, PT cut to NT$79. 2026/27/28
EPS revised by +46%/+10%/-25%.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

WT Micro: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target to NT$174: We update our residual income model to reflect
earnings estimate revisions. We reduce our terminal growth rate assumption from 3%  to
2%, in line with longer term global macro economic growth rate. We cut our intermediate
growth rate assumption from 8.0% to 4.0% to reflect demand uncertainties in the end
market. We also raise our beta estimate from 0.90 to 1.10 to reflect potentially peaking

growth and demand uncertainties, and this leads to an increased cost of equity from 13.3%
to 15.6%. Other key RI assumptions are unchanged: 2.0% risk-free rate, and  cash dividend
payout ratio of 85%. We believe WT deserves a high beta due to the high liabilities to
equity ratio (5.92x as of 3Q26) and the strong correlation with the wider Taiwan stock
market due to the nature of the distribution business.
We downgrade WT to EW. We believe the risk factors we’re seeing are likely to persist

and the stock price will likely remain under pressure unless there is further clarity or
policy reversals; we do not recommend accumulation even though the stock price has
corrected significantly from its peak in May.

WPG: Estimate Revisions Summary
We revise 2026-28e EPS by +46%/+10%/-25% each:   This factors in 2-3Q26 actual results
and 4Q26 guidance, which led to stronger 2026 estimates, as well as some near-term
resilience into 2027 with sustained memory shipment volume. Further into 2027 and
2028, we factor in an increasingly uncertain investment environment and therefore reduce
our estimates for non-operating income, mainly investment income.

### Page 8

M
Idea
8
WPG: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target to NT$79: We update our residual income model to reflect
earnings estimate revisions. We also raise our beta estimate from 1.3 to 1.5 to reflect
potentially peaking growth and demand uncertainties, and raised our risk premium from
6% to 8% to reflect market volatilities; this leads to an increased cost of equity from 9.5%
to 13.9%. We also raise our risk free rate from 1.5% to 2.0% to reflect rising funding costs.

Our intermediate growth rate is reduced from 5.5% to 4.0% to reflect demand
uncertainties. Other key RI assumptions are unchanged: cash dividend payout ratio of
80%, and long-term growth rate of 2.0%. We believe WPG deserves a high beta due to its
high liabilities to equity ratio (5.85x as of 3Q26) and the strong correlation to the wider
Taiwan stock market due to the nature of the distribution business.
We downgrade WPG to EW. We believe the risk factors we’re seeing are likely to persist

and the stock price will likely remain under pressure unless there is further clarity or
policy reversals; we do not recommend accumulation even though the stock price has
corrected significantly from its peak in August.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

chrischenlc@yuanta.com

InP 基板缺貨影響光通訊，3Q26 營運低於預期且 4Q 估計下修 
3Q26 營收 3.2 億元，季增 25%且年增 27%；毛利率/營益率為 39%/18%，
季增 4.7 ppts/9.7 ppts，EPS 1.26 元，季增 214%/年增 68%，低於預期 23%；
光通訊占比為 25%，低於 1Q26/2Q26 的 45%/37%。光通訊比重低使毛利率
較原估計低 3.9 ppts。估計 4Q26 營收 3.77 億元，季增 18% (10 月營收已達
1.24 億元)，下修 14%，主因 InP 基板持續缺貨中；毛利率下修 4.4ppts 至
38.8%；EPS 估計為 1.31 元，下修 38%。 
GaAs 應用之研發案增加，包含 VCSEL、QD Laser 及 PIN 
IET-KY 的 MBE 磊晶包含 GaAs(砷化鎵)、InP(磷化銦)、GaSb(銻化鎵)等材
料，雖然 InP 現階段基板不足，惟 GaAs 的研發案近期有所斬獲，如(1) 射頻
用於國防；(2) VCSEL，200G 射頻用於光通訊(市場尚未有量產者)；(3) 
QD(量子點)雷射，發射端已於 2026 年中送樣再調整結構中；(4) PIN，用於
光通訊的接收端。此外，GaSb(銻化鎵)估計 2026 年增 65%且占比 19%以國
防應用為主，近期亦有新的商業需求正與客戶洽談中。 
2026 及 2027 年 EPS 估計下修 31%及 29%，目前評價合理 
IET-KY 目前光通訊出貨以 100G 及 200G 的 PD 為主；公司無採用上游中國
大陸設廠之 AXT 的 InP 基板，目前採日系基板為主且今年處於即到貨即用的
緊俏狀態。德系供應商期待後續產能提升但仍需時間，本中心估計 2026 年
光通訊占比由 2025 年的 44%降至 35%，2027 年回到占比 44%。本中心下
修估計 2026/2027 年營收 13%/13%至 12.85 億/18.61 億元，年增 19%/45%，
毛利率 40%/43%，下修 3.5/4.7 百分點，EPS 為 4.63 元/9.03 元，年增
184%/95%，下修 31%/29%。因 InP 基板取得不確定性而下修目標本益比，
採估計 2027 年 EPS 以 45 倍目標本益比(前次採 57 倍)給予目標價 410 元，
目前評價合理給予持有之投資評等。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
InP 基板缺貨影響使 3Q26 獲利低於預期 23%至 EPS 1.26 元，且光通
訊占比為 25%，低於 1Q26/2Q26 的 45%/37%；4Q 估 EPS 1.31 元。 
⧫  
GaAs 應用之研發案增加，包含 VCSEL、QD Laser 及 PIN，有機會於
2028 年貢獻；此外，GaSb 於國防及商業需求均提升。 
⧫  
採日系基板為主且處於即到貨即用的緊俏狀態；2026 及 2027 年 EPS
估計下修 31%及 29%至 4.6 及 9.0 元，呈高度成長惟目前評價合理。

### Page 3

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：半導體 2026 年 11 月 17 日 IET 第3 頁，共 14頁
上游 InP 基板緊缺難以完全解決，惟公司本身可望於 2H27 稍改善
IET-KY 目前光通訊出貨以 100G 及 200G 的 PD 為主，InP 基板短缺影響營運；因公司若干產品用
於美國軍工國防，故無採用上游中國大陸設廠之 AXT 的 InP 基板，目前採日系基板比重達 80%，
而且今年處於即到貨即用的緊俏狀態。德系供應商期待後續產能提升，且部份客戶自行提供基板；
然而整體基板於市場供需仍然緊俏，其他磊晶廠商亦積極搶占基板料源，基板供應商均已規劃大幅
度擴充產能惟仍需時間，估計二年內均呈緊缺狀況，公司則於 2H27 稍微改善。
GaAs(砷化鎵)應用之研發案增加，GaSb(銻化鎵)亦有新的商業需求
IET-KY 的 MBE 磊晶包含 GaAs(砷化鎵)、InP(磷化銦)、GaSb(銻化鎵)等材料，雖然 InP 現階段基
板不足，惟 GaAs 的研發案近期有所斬獲，如(1)射頻用於國防；(2)VCSEL，200G 射頻用於光通訊

(市場尚未有量產者)；(3)QD(量子點)雷射，發射端已於 2026 年中送樣再調整結構中；(4)PIN，用
於光通訊的接收端。此外，GaSb(銻化鎵)估計 2026 年增 65%以國防應用為主，近期亦有新的商業
需求正與客戶洽談中。
光聖換股已完成，3Q26 於業外挹注股利收入 0.16 億元
IET-KY 與光聖換股已於 5 月完成，透過股份交換結盟後，雙方相互持股比例分別為光聖持有 IET-
KY 約 15.26% 普通股股權，IET-KY 持有光聖約 1.79% 普通股股權。3Q26 於業外挹注股利收入
0.16 億元，短期尚未有其他本業的助益，長期有機會與其子公司合作開發產品。
MBE 磊晶在厚度控制及品質具優勢，估 2026/2027 年營運明顯年增但較先前保守
IET-KY 營運主體位於美國德州，以 MBE(Molecular Beam Epitaxy；分子束磊晶)技術為核心，同時
具備機器設備開發能力。MBE 磊晶在厚度控制及品質上準確度高於 MOCVD，適合新品研發及高階

產品。高階光模組應用於 2026 年以 400G 及 800G 為主流，多採用 100G 的 PD。現階段 IET 出貨
以 100G PD 及 200G PD(可用於 200G 光模組)為主。公司 MBE 機台為 16 台，估 2027 年增至 19
台。由於 InP 基板缺貨難解，本中心下修 2026/2027 年營收 13%/13%至 12.85 億/18.61 億元，年
增 19%/45%，毛利率 40%/43%，下修 3.5/4.7 百分點，EPS 為 4.63 元/9.03 元，年增 184%/95%，
下修 31%/29%。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：半導體 2026 年 11 月 17 日 IET 第4 頁，共 14頁  
圖 4：2025 年各季營收逐步走高，主要來自光通訊應用的貢獻

資料來源：公司資料、元大投顧整理

圖 5：2025、2026F 及 2027F 年估計營收及營益率逐年走高

資料來源：公司資料、元大投顧預估 
MBE 透過分子束提高均勻性、純度等，高階光通訊趨勢有利營運增長 
MBE (Molecular Beam Epitaxy)分子束磊晶製程為：(1)分子束形成：將坩鍋中的原料透過噴射源精
確加熱以昇華、蒸發分子形成分子束，分子如砷、鎵、磷、銦，噴嘴以石英克努森容器（Kundsen 
Cell）為主；(2)表面附著與擴散：分子束抵達加熱過的基板表面並吸附後將在晶格能量最低的地方
進行鍵結；(3)外延生長：透過精確控制基板溫度與分子束通量，薄膜會依照基板的晶體結構進行層
層堆疊最終形成磊晶。 
噴射源重要性在於透過熱管理、快門（Shutter）控制提供穩定、均勻的分子束，使薄膜品質穩定。
MBE 另外一大重要的設備為反射高能電子繞射儀 (Refection High-Energy Electron Diffraction, 
RHEED)，透過 RHEED 可以進行生長速率控制、生長狀態監控以即時控管磊晶品質。 
MBE 相較於 MOCVD 的優勢於：(1)精準度：透過分子束的控制更精準的製造且控制厚度；(2)減少
雜質：在原料端由於減去了前驅物的使用使材料純度更高，且基板遷移摻雜問題較小。然而，生長
速度較慢與且耗材要求較高，整體成本較高。

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：半導體 2026 年 11 月 17 日 IET 第5 頁，共 14頁  
產業概況  
光收發模組明顯成長且 800G 及 1.6T 零件更高階；NPO/CPO 數量低但高成長 
AI 對高速網路傳輸速率的需求持續攀升，光收發模組 (Optical Transceiver) 扮演關鍵角色；光收發
模組於 400G 以下且市場穩定；然而 400G 以上估年增 2026/2027 年為 80%/43%至 1 億/1.45 億個；
2026 年主流為 400G，2027 及 2028 年主流為 800G 且未來升級至 1.6T 以上則採更高階零組件，
如 Interposer、TEC 及 Submount 等電子級精密陶瓷。NPO/CPO 仍為初始階段，2026/2027/2028
年估 40 萬/320 萬/2,200 萬個：2027/2028 年增 680%/595%，惟數量遠低於光收發模組；CPO 將
OE 與交換機的 ASIC 共同封裝，降低傳輸功耗至 1/3；NPO 僅將 OE 與 ASIC 放置在交換機內同一
PCB 基板上，降低交換機傳輸功耗至 50%；二者均需插拔式 ELSFP 模組。

插拔式光收發模組以光晶片、PCB 及 DSP 最短缺 
插拔式光收發模組以光晶片、PCB 及 DSP 最短缺；內部架構以 DSP 為主晶片，其與 TIA 及 Driver
組成 EIC；PIC 則包含 PD、 LD、調變器； OE(光引擎)包含 EIC 與 PIC，所有零件在傳輸速率提升
下持續升級。PCB 在高階光模組需 mSAP 製程具門檻， DSP 由國際少數廠商寡占使供需相當緊俏，
內部的發射及接收端光元件晶粒以國際大廠為主。

圖 8：光收發模組供應商、以插拔式光模組為主(左)，CPO/NPO 市場仍小但成長性高(右)

資料來源：網路資料、元大投顧整理

圖 9：光收發模組中的 OE (左)；CPO/NPO 將 OE 置交換機內與 ASIC 共同封裝/同一 PCB 板 (右)

資料來源：市場資料、元大投顧整理

資料傳輸更高速使光晶片數量及規格提升；CPO/NPO 亦需插拔式光模組 
AI 交換機(Switch) 單埠（Port）的傳輸資料量主要取決於交換機的世代技術，2026 年主流為單 Port 
800G 的傳輸速率採 8 通道且單埠每秒可傳輸約 100 GB 的資料；1.6T（1.6 Tbps）採 8 通道且單
埠每秒可傳輸達 200 GB 的資料；未來若發展 3.2T 將採 8 通道且單埠每秒可傳輸達 400 GB 的資
料。 
此外，CPO（Co-Packaged Optics；共同封裝光學）光子積體電路（PIC）與電子積體電路（EIC）
共同封裝在同一個載板上，目前市場的 CPO/NPO 之 AI 交換機(Switch)，採 ELSFP（External 
Laser Small Form Pluggable，外接雷射光源模組），調變晶片靠近 ASIC 位置以縮短傳輸距離及降
低功耗；ELSFP 將容易受温度影響且壽命較短的雷射光源獨立出來，放在主機板邊緣作為標準化
插拔模組，不僅提升散熱效率，也解決 CPO/NPO 系統的維護難題。

### Page 7

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：半導體 2026 年 11 月 17 日 IET 第7 頁，共 14頁
獲利調整與股票評價
MBE 磊晶法於高階應用具前景故提升機台產能，惟目前評價合理
IET-KY 為美國製造的 MBE(Molecular Beam Epitaxy；分子束磊晶)製程 III-V 族化合物磊晶廠，光
通訊(光纖網路)產品以應用於光收發模組為主要動能，占營收比重 2024 年的 29%升至 2025 年的
44%，帶動 2025 年毛利率年增 20 百分點至 36%。IET 的 100G 及 200G PD 可應用於 800G 及 1.6T
的光收發模組；未來市場朝 3.2T 邁進時需要 400G PD 且公司已進入研發階段，惟 2029 年後才有
顯著成長動能。
公司 MBE 機台為 16 台，估 2027 年增至 19 台。由於 InP 基板缺貨難解，本中心估計 2026 年光通
訊占比由 2025 年的 44%降至 35%，金額年減 5%；2027 年回到占比 44%，金額年增 79%。本中

心下修估計 2026/2027 年營收 13%/13%至 12.85 億/18.61 億元，年增 19%/45%，毛利率 40%/43%，
下修 3.5/4.7 百分點，EPS 為 4.63 元/9.03 元，年增 184%/95%，下修 31%/29%。因 InP 基板取得
不確定性而下修目標本益比，採估計 2027 年 EPS 以 45 倍目標本益比(前次採 57 倍)給予目標價
410 元，目前評價合理給予持有之投資評等。

公司簡介  
生產基地位於美國的Ⅲ-Ⅴ族化合物半導體 MBE 磊晶製造商，光通訊比重最高且持續攀升 
IET-KY 成立於 2011 年，營運主體位於美國德州，為Ⅲ-Ⅴ族化合物半導體磊晶片代工廠，以 MBE 磊晶技術為核心，產品
包含 GaAs(砷化鎵)、InP(磷化銦)，GaSb(銻化鎵)；應用領域包含無線通訊、衛星通訊、光纖通訊、夜視鏡、保全監控、太
空、軍事、生物醫學、資料傳輸、雲端試算等，光通訊 PD 磊晶為近期主要成長動能。

1 20260327 526.00 650 650 買進 陳玫芬 
2 20260508 750.00 855 855 持有-超越同業 陳玫芬

資料來源：CMoney、元大投顧 
註：A = 未調整之收盤價；B = 未調整之目標價； C = 依據股本變動調整後之目標價。員工分紅稀釋影響未反映於 A、B 或 C。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

AI 高階測試需求維持強勁，成長趨勢不變 
◼ 產品組合有望優化，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元：CPC 及 VPC
稼動率皆維持高檔，預估 4Q26 營收 63.95 億元(+66.7%YoY)。獲利
方面，受惠產品組合持續優化，預估毛利率季增至 61.92%，隨營收
規模持續擴大，費用率有望年減至 25%，營益率 37.12%，稅後淨
利 20.75 億元(+119%YoY)，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元。 
◼ 產能擴充計畫持續推進，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 68.44 元：展望後
市，隨 AI HPC 需求持續成長，探針卡朝高頻高速、大電流及高針腳
數方向發展，帶動高階測試需求維持強勁。為解決產能瓶頸，旺矽
將積極擴充產能，2026/2027 年底 MEMS 月產能為 350 萬/600 萬
針，VPC 月產能為 200 萬/250 萬針，且 PCB 廠房預計 2H27 投產，
有助提升 PCB 自製率。整體而言，預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 
68.44/118.84 元，且 CPO 相關設備有望於 2028 年較明顯挹注營
收，預估 2028 年稅後 EPS 204.56 元。 
◼ 維持買進評等，目標價 6,500 元：評價方面，以 2028 年 EPS 估算，
目前 PE 約為 21X，低於旺矽近 2 年 30-55X，考量 AI 高階測試需求
持續強勁，且 CPO 布局有望挹注中長期成長動能，長線維持買進評
等，目標價調整為 6,500 元(2028F PE 32X)。

旺矽為全球前五大探針卡廠商  旺矽 1995 年 7 月於新竹成立，實收資本額 9.8 億元，截至 Apr-26，員工人數
共 2,110 人。主要業務包含晶圓探針卡測試及半導體設備銷售。旺矽提供一站
式購足服務，探針卡業務包含懸臂式探針卡(Cantilever Probe Cards)、垂直式
探針卡(Vertical Probe Cards)及微機電探針卡(MEMS)等；半導體設備則例如
光電自動化設備(PA)、溫控測試設備(Thermal)、先進半導體測試設備
(Advanced Semiconductor Test, AST)等。以 3Q26 產品別營收結構而言，探
針卡 72.7%，設備 25.4%，其他 1.9%。

產品組合有望優化，預估 4Q26
稅後 EPS 21.18 元 
 由於 AI HPC 客 戶 拉 貨 動 能 持 續 強 勁 ， 旺 矽 Oct-26 營收 22.32 億元
(+8.98%MoM，+92.73%YoY)，優於預期。考量 CPC 及 VPC 稼動率皆維持高
檔，預估 4Q26 營收 63.95 億元(+8.3%QoQ，+66.7%YoY)。獲利方面，受惠
產品組合持續優化，預估毛利率季增至 61.92%，隨營收規模持續擴大，費用
率有望年減至 25%，營益率 37.12%，稅後淨利 20.75 億元(+10.4%QoQ，
+119%YoY)，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元。

產能擴充計畫持續推進，預 估
2026 年稅後 EPS 68.44 元

展望後市，隨 AI 與 HPC 需求持續成長，探針卡朝高頻高速、大電流及高針腳
數方向發展，帶動高階測試需求維持強勁。為解決產能瓶頸，旺矽將積極擴充
產能，包含：MEMS 月產能自 2025 年底 100 萬針，預期 2026 年底提升至 350
萬針，2027 年底目標為 600 萬針；VPC 月產能則自 2025 年底 120 萬針，預
期 2026 年底提升為 200 萬針，2027 年底有望再增加至 250 萬針；CPC 月產
能則維持持平略增。廠房方面，旺矽 Nov-26 中旬公告投入 22 億元進行湖口廠
機電工程，PCB 廠房預計如期於 2H27 投產，有助提升 PCB 自製率；另，
CPO 設備所需廠房改建，預計 1Q27 完成。資本支出方面，預估 2026/2027 年
分別為 15-18 億元及 20 億元，2027 年主要用於設備採購。

整體而言，投顧預估 2026 年營收 214.7 億元(+60.5%YoY)，受惠產品組合持
續優化，毛利率 60.5%，營益率 36%，稅後淨利 67.1 億元(+111.1%YoY)，上
修 2026 年稅後 EPS 至 68.44 元(原預估 64.97 元)。

Exhibit 6：旺矽擴產時程推估

Sources: Company，Taishin 
高階測試需求續強，預估 2027
年稅後 EPS 118.84 元 
 旺矽自 2026 年起整併 LED 與 AST 部門，並持續布局 CPO 檢測專案，目前主
要聚焦於 CPO insertion 2 之 EIC/PIC 測試及 insertion 3 之 die-level 測試應
用。由於 CPO 測試涉及光電整合，技術門檻及客製化程度較高，目前仍持續
與客戶進行驗證及開發。其中，insertion 2 既有客戶預計 2026 年底更新驗證
進度，另有新客戶持續接洽中，相關進展有望於 1H27 較為明朗；insertion 3
則已有多家國際客戶出貨工程機，且持續交機中，有望 1H27 逐步轉為量產機
需求。展望後市，隨 AI 高速傳輸需求提升，CPO 相關測試設備有望成為 AST
業務中長期成長動能，惟考量設備交期較長及客戶驗收認證所需時間，預期
2028 年營收貢獻將較為顯著，CPO 相關業務營收占比有望達雙位數。

維持買進評等，目標價 6,500 元

評價方面，以 2028 年 EPS 估算，目前 PE 約為 21X，低於旺矽近 2 年 30-
55X，考量 AI 高階測試需求持續強勁，且 CPO 布局有望挹注中長期成長動
能，長線維持買進評等，目標價調整為 6,500 元(2028F PE 32X)。

風險提示  1) 客戶拉貨不如預期；2) 新品研發不如預期；3) 匯率變化。

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상 
(단위: 십억원, %)

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: risks mount
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
10:31 November 20 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October, employment falling 43.5k following
September’s 36.4k decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the year have now slowed
to 1.1%yr, materially below current growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the

unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9% despite the participation rate falling
another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

### Page 2

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer momentum in hiring, now we are seeing
evidence of a material increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have remained in
employment to date, but persistent job shedding raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend
in the unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most
certainly before the late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3
increase was skewed towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay outcomes directly
tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements
meanwhile, the most responsive to current and expected labour market conditions, accounted for their
smallest share of a Q3 wage increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour cost

growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident in housing. The Westpac Housing
Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005. Interestingly,
there remains considerable latent demand: almost 13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over
the next year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak sentiment, high interest rates and

expectations of further price declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These factors also
pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for inflation remained the critical theme. The
October FOMC minutes highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs and energy
prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several [members] judg[ing] that price pressures were
increasingly extending beyond industries directly affected by data center construction”. Participants

"assessed that medium- and longer-term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-target inflation is a threat to the status
quo, and perceived risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke throughout the week
also kept the focus on price risks, and called for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again
proposed a 50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at the December and January

meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales
gave further justification for this view this week, the control group surprising to the upside with a 0.6%
gain in October after a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said, housing is under
considerable pressure, pending home sales falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back

at 2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent weakness in this sector will, in time, have
implications for both consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international development of the week. Again, it provided
a call for pro-active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr
and the decline in fixed asset investment broadened outside property, with capital expenditure now

down 8.1% across the economy year-to-date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth; though, even here, momentum softened
in October, annual growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not only activity that

### Page 3

Related articles
remains under pressure, but also wealth, with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective, stimulus needs to affect change in both activity
and wealth and do so in a sustainable manner.

Document 317: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-23).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

#datax #interestrates #consumer #australia #tax #data #rba
Consumer Panel update Q3 2026: More resilience
By Illiana Jain
Economist, Westpac
11:11 November 03 2026 Share
The Q3 update from the Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel unpacks spending and savings
trends across Westpac's broad customer base.
Click here Q3 Consumer Panel report (PDF 520KB)

### Page 2

Related articles
The Q3 Consumer Panel shows household spending remains resilient despite renewed pressure from
fuel costs. Spending rose 1.5%qtr, with discretionary categories contributing 0.9ppts to growth
compared with 0.3ppts from fuel. Savings also rose 1.3%qtr, lifting average mortgage buffers to 22.2
months.
Beneath the aggregate resilience, pressures remain uneven. Low-buffer mortgagors face higher
repayments and fuel costs with substantially smaller savings cushions, yet their discretionary spending

has continued to converge towards better-buffered households

Document 339: First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-23).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

M: +64 21 710 852

It has been a diﬃcult week for metals. Copper is down more
than 8%, aluminium almost 6% and gold around 9% as a
stronger dollar, higher rate expectations and weaker risk
appetite weigh on the complex
Ewa Manthey
Metals have come under sharp pressure this week, despite continued tightness
in physical markets
The speed of the sell-oﬀ looks more like a sharp repricing of investor
exposure than a broad weakening in physical markets. Copper has
taken the biggest hit as investors unwind bullish positions and

reassess AI-related demand. Yet the sharp correction in prices
contrasts with still-tight physical markets.

Investor selling weighs on copper
Copper is on track for one of its steepest weekly declines in a
decade.
We think the speed of the sell-oﬀ partly reﬂects an unwind of bullish
investor positioning. Both LME and COMEX net length have fallen
sharply from recent highs, while positioning across the wider
metals complex has also become less bullish.

Copper has also become more closely correlated with equity
markets, including AI-related stocks. The AI link matters for investor
sentiment, but it does not make copper's demand story dependent
on data centres alone. Grid investment and electriﬁcation remain
important sources of demand. But as investors cut exposure to AI-
related assets, copper has become more vulnerable to the same
selling pressure.

Copper could remain under pressure if funds continue to cut

correction has moved much faster than physical conditions. Tight
availability in China should provide some support once the investor
selling eases.

tightness now supporting the physical market.
Aluminium supply remains tight for now

Aluminium has fallen with the wider complex, but nearby supply
remains tight. The LME cash-to-three-month spread has moved
into a steep backwardation, while LME stocks remain historically
low and largely of Russian origin, limiting what is readily available
to some buyers. This should help contain the near-term downside.
At the same time, investors have reduced bullish aluminium
positions from recent highs, in line with the broader move across
metals.

The medium-term picture is less supportive. Middle Eastern
production is recovering, and new Indonesian capacity is coming
online. We expect the market to move towards surplus next year,
which should limit the scope for a sustained price recovery.

Gold feels the pressure from higher rates
Gold has fallen for ﬁve straight sessions and is down around 9%

this week, putting it on course for its steepest weekly decline since
March. Prices have also touched their lowest level since September
2025.
The shift in the macro backdrop has weighed on gold, with higher
rate expectations and a stronger dollar reducing its appeal. The
equity sell-oﬀ may also have prompted some investors to sell gold
to raise cash.
We expect gold to remain vulnerable while rate expectations and
the dollar remain elevated. Central bank demand remains an

important longer-term support, with China adding 26 tonnes to its
reserves in October. But for now, macro forces are likely to set the
direction.

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂

Neuralink 近⽇发布视频，展⽰⼀名失去双臂功能的瘫痪患者凭借植⼊的 N1 脑机接⼝芯⽚，
仅⽤意念便在新作《 GTA 6 》中完成驾驶快艇、⽔上⻜机及格⽃等复杂操作，彻底告别此
前 “ 嘴含棍⼦戳 iPad” 的困境。
摘要：
⻢斯克旗下脑机接⼝公司 Neuralink 再度展⽰其技术突破 —— ⼀名失去双臂功能的瘫痪患者，借
助植⼊式芯⽚，仅凭意念便在热⻔新作《 GTA 6 》中驾驶快艇、驾驶⽔上⻜机并完成徒⼿格⽃。
Neuralink 近⽇在其 X 账号发布相关视频，记录了上述患者全程⽤脑机接⼝（ BCI ）操控游戏的过
程。这名患者表⽰，⼿术前只能靠 " 嘴含棍⼦戳 iPad" 来玩游戏，⽽植⼊芯⽚后的感受是 " 你⽴刻
重新拥有了通往世界的⼊⼝ " 。
《 GTA 6 》本周正式发售，预售销量已达 3250 万份。 Neuralink 选择在此节点发布视频，进⼀步

凸显其技术的实⽤价值，也为这家初创公司在公众视野中积累了新的曝光。
从 " 啄⻝ iPad" 到意念操控
据 Neuralink 发布的视频，这名患者在植⼊ N1 芯⽚后，得以通过⼤脑信号直接操作游戏，完成了
快艇驾驶、⽔上⻜机⻜⾏及⾁搏战等复杂操作。他描述，使⽤嘴棍操控 iPad 的旧⽅式令他 " 像⼀
只⻦⼀样不停地啄 " ，⽽脑机接⼝的引⼊从根本上改变了这⼀体验。
这并⾮ Neuralink ⾸次展⽰其在游戏⽆障碍领域的能⼒。 2024 年 3 ⽉， Neuralink 曾公开第⼀位接
受植⼊⼿术的⼈类患者 Noland Arbaugh 通过芯⽚操控国际象棋、《⽂明 VI 》及《⻢⾥奥卡丁⻋》
的画⾯。据报道，尽管此后部分连接电极出现脱落， Arbaugh 对游戏的控制能⼒仍持续提升。
需求远超供给，技术普及仍⾯临制约
游戏控制之外， Neuralink 今年已取得多项重要进展。今年 3 ⽉，该公司宣布成功帮助⼀名患有渐
冻症（ ALS ）的患者 Kenneth Shock ，将其脑电信号实时转化为可被听到的语⾳ —— 这是

Neuralink 在语⾔辅助领域迈出的关键⼀步。 ALS 可导致严重的⾔语障碍，⽽该技术为此类患者
提供了全新的沟通途径。
尽管技术层⾯持续取得突破， Neuralink ⽬前的覆盖能⼒仍⼗分有限。据报道，截⾄ 2025 年底，
已有约 1 万名有意愿接受 N1 芯⽚植⼊的患者排队等候。按照公司现⾏标准，申请者须因颈椎脊髓
损伤或 ALS 导致⼿部功能严重受损乃⾄丧失，⽅可获得资格。
这意味着，对于数以万计仍在等待的残障⽤⼾⽽⾔，脑机接⼝技术在短期内尚⽆法形成规模化
解 决 ⽅ 案 。 技 术 突 破 的 ⽰ 范 效 应 固 然 ⿎ 舞 ⼈ ⼼ ， 但 从 实 验 室 成 果 到 ⼴ 泛 临 床 应 ⽤ 之 间 ，
Neuralink 仍⾯临较⻓的路要⾛。

Inflationary pressure fades as we move through year-end

Key points
• October CPI to rise 0.41% with the annual

• October Trimmed Mean (TM) is set

• The December quarter will represent the

and the TM is unchanged at 0.7%qtr.

• Demand remains the larger driver of 
inflation, but the recent pick-up in 
trimmed mean inflation appears to 
have been largely supply-driven.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Inflation accelerated in September...
The CPI rose 0.8% in the month of September, stronger than 
Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the market’s expectation of 
0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr. The 
seasonally adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting 
some positive seasonality in the month. 
Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: auto 
fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday travel & accommodation 
(8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation 
(7.2%mth); and property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There 
were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–3.1%mth); 
household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The more important underlying measure, the Trimmed Mean 
(TM), gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and 
market expectations with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr 
(revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised 
pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this 
series.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second 
consecutive month. The share of the basket running above 
3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, while the share below 2%yr 
rose from 27% to 32%. Meanwhile, our replication of the San 
Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum index moved into 
positive territory after trending higher since April. Around 
14% of the CPI basket, on an expenditure-weighted basis, 
experienced positive inflation shocks in September, driven 
by holiday travel, fuel, and clothing & footwear. This was 
partly offset by negative shocks across 7% of the basket, 
concentrated in food & non-alcoholic beverages. For full 
analysis of the September CPI, see “ Inflation accelerating in 
the run up to Christmas”.
... but is set to peak in October.
Looking ahead to October, early indicators were mixed. The 
NAB business survey suggested firms continued to absorb 
a meaningful share of higher input costs, with purchase cost 
growth edging up to 2.4%qtr (+0.1ppt from September), while 
retail price growth slowed sharply to 0.2%qtr (–0.8ppts), 
partly reflecting some moderation in labour cost growth 
(see here). Meanwhile, consumers’ year-ahead inflation 
expectations rose to 5.3% in October, with this rate holding 
steady in November. Fuel prices provided a more pronounced 
source of inflationary pressure, with retail petrol and diesel 
prices jumping 8.7% and 5.3%, respectively, in October to 
average $2.40/l and $2.82/l as global oil prices approached a 
local peak.
October to mark the peak in inflation
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.4% (0.41% to 2dp), lifting
annual inflation to 4.8%, which we expect to mark the peak.
On a seasonally adjusted basis, we estimate a stronger 0.7%
increase (0.68% to 2dp). For the monthly trimmed mean, we

expect a 0.4% rise (0.42% to 2dp), nudging the annual pace up
to 3.7%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking a 1.0% increase
in October. While conceptually different from the quarterly
trimmed mean, this measure nonetheless provides a useful
guide to near-term underlying inflation momentum. For market
services excluding volatiles & holiday travel, we have pencilled
in a 0.3% rise, lifting annual inflation to 4.0% from 3.8%,
which had marked a ten-month low.

Transport is expected to make the largest contribution
(+0.35ppts), almost entirely reflecting auto fuel (+0.32ppts).
Smaller contributions are expected from clothing & footwear
(+0.06ppts), household goods & services (+0.05ppts), and
meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The main offset comes
from recreation & culture (–0.18ppts), driven entirely by
holiday travel (–0.18ppts), while fruit & vegetables, utilities
and several other components provide only marginal offsets.

Key details for the month of October
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to rise 0.2% in
October, lifting annual inflation to 3.3%. Strength in the month
is concentrated in meals out & takeaway, where we expect
prices to rise 0.6%mth and 4.3%yr. We have allowed for a
greater-than-usual degree of price resetting as businesses
adjust to the card surcharging ban.
Housing inflation is expected to rise 0.1% in October, with the
annual pace increasing to 6.2% reflecting base effects from

electricity. Across the major components:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.2%mth and 3.5%yr, with the
late-September indexation of the Commonwealth Rent
Assistance flowing through more broadly in October. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate is stronger at 0.4%mth.
• New dwelling inflation has been a key source of downside
surprise in recent months. We expect a 0.2%mth rise
in October, which would see the annual pace moderate
to 4.5%. The downturn in established home prices has

deepened into year-end (see here), which should weigh on
new dwelling inflation over the months ahead. Our current
estimates point to a trough of around 1.3%yr by late-2027.
• Maintenance & repair costs are expected rise a solid
0.9%mth, taking annual inflation to 3.5%, while gas & other
household fuels provide the main offset, falling 2.5%mth.
Transport is expected to be the main source of upward
pressure in October, rising 2.9%mth and lifting to 11.6%yr.

This largely reflects an 8.2%mth jump in auto fuel prices
following the rise in global oil prices through the month. Motor
vehicle prices and spare parts are also expected to increase, by
0.4%mth and 1.6%mth respectively.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Recreation & culture is expected to provide the key offset, 
falling 1.5%mth, driven by a 3.0%mth decline in holiday travel. 
We also expect some pull-back across components exposed 
to the AI trade, including audio, visual & computing equipment 
(–0.2%mth) and games, toys & hobbies (–0.3%mth).
Underlying inflation holds momentum
As noted above, our October TM estimate is also 0.4%mth, a step 
up from 0.3% in September taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 
3.6%yr and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 
4.0%yr, the fastest pace in the short history of the series. Rents 
and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
Upside risks to October baseline ...
We view the risks around the October CPI outcome as slightly 
tilted to the upside. Our estimates already allow for a greater 
degree of price resetting in areas such as meals out & takeaway 
and personal and household services, but there is a risk that this 
adjustment proves broader than we have assumed. Increases in 
fuel levy surcharges in early October are another source of upside 
risk, including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post, with 
the latter nearly doubling its surcharge between September and 
October. The key downside risk remains housing, particularly 
new dwelling prices, which have surprised to the downside in 
recent months and could continue to do so as the housing market 
downturn deepens. Trade notifications data also point to some 
further downside risk, with builders on average announcing 
smaller price changes than in prior months.
... but momentum to ease in November ...
We have a preliminary estimate for the November CPI of 
–0.1%mth which will see the annual pace ease back to 4.6%yr. 
Auto fuel moved lower through November to be down –2.1% in 
the month with seasonal falls in garments, health, furniture & 
household appliances and holiday travel offsetting some strength 
in food, housing, communication and financial services. 
Seasonally adjusted the CPI is estimated to lift 0.1% in November, 
highlighting the seasonal weakness in the month, while our TM 
estimate is for a 0.2% lift seeing the annual pace ease a touch to 
3.6%yr while the six month annualised pace drops from 4.2%yr 
to 3.9%yr. This moderation is expected to continue into December 
with a preliminary TM estimate of 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. 
The following page has a table with monthly profiles for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to July 2027. By July 
2027 we now expect an annual pace of 3.3%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI while our estimate for the TM is 3.0%yr. 
More significantly, the six month annualised pace of the TM is 
estimated to be down to 2.7%yr by June 2027, well within the 
RBA’s target band. We have also included tables breaking down 
the monthly components of the CPI through the December and 
March quarters. 
... with December quarter now the peak ...
Our December quarter CPI estimate has been revised down 
slightly to 1.2%qtr, a modest 0.05ppt revision, with the annual 
pace lifting from 3.9%yr to 4.6%yr. The seasonally adjusted

CPI gains 1.4% in the December quarter highlighting normally
December is a seasonally soft quarter. Our TM estimate for
Q3 has a small downward revision of 0.05ppt to 0.85% at two
decimal place but this rounds up to our published estimate of
0.9%qtr. For now that suggests downside risk for our TM

estimate as getting the quarter to round to 0.8% is much
easier than getting it to round up to 1.0% . We also note
auto fuel prices falling faster than expected which may not
only result in downside risks to out CPI estimate but also our
TM estimate due to less pass through pressure.
... with inflation moderating through 2027 ...
Westpac’s preliminary March quarter CPI estimate is just 0.4%.
Driving most of this outcome is the 7.7% decline in autofuel

(–0.29ppt) and a 3.8% fall in holiday travel (–0.23ppt) being
offset by rising food (1.0%, 0.18ppt), housing (0.7%, 0.15ppt),
health (2.6%, 0.18ppt) and education (3.5%, 0.17ppt). The
March quarter does not have a lot in the way of net seasonally
with the seasonally adjusted estimate up 0.5%. Our underlying
TM measure of inflation is estimated to rise 0.7% in the March
quarter taking the annual pace down to 3.5% while the six
month annualised pace dips from 3.8%yr to 3.2%yr.

... as a disinflationary impulse kicks in.
We have for some time expected auto fuel prices to peak in
October, 240 cpl for petrol and 282 cpl for diesel, then to
trend lower from there. Our November estimates are currently
sitting on 237 cpl and 279 cpl respectively trending down to
186 cpl for petrol and 196 cpl for diesel by December 2027.
This represents a 12.7% fall in auto fuel through the first half
of 2027 subtracting 0.42ppt off the CPI over the same period

of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the
auto fuels profile please see: “Persistent energy pressures
have further to run”. At this stage we have not adjusted our
Brent forecast in line with the current correction underway
(see the November Market Outlook for more details why) as
we expect this to be a volatile ride. Should the current correc-
tion continue, auto fuel will represent a larger drag on the CPI
and potentially the Trimmed Mean.

Critical to our expected moderation in core inflation is a step
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab-
lished home prices. From a 1.0%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we
see dwelling price increasing by 0.4% in Q4 2026, and then
0.5% in Q1 and 0.4%qtr in Q2 before dropping to 0.2%qtr in
Q3 and Q4. This will see dwelling price inflation in the CPI
drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 2.3%yr by June 2027 then

down to 1.3%yr by December 2027.
At least to the end of this year there is still a high degree of
uncertainty about how much businesses will be able to pass
through upstream cost pressures. The latest NAB business sur-
vey continues to highlight robust input price pressure but also

that firms are struggling to pass on those increases.
For businesses, it is almost a now or never opportunity to raise
prices. We expect that through 2027, as energy prices ease
and the domestic economy slows under the weight of higher
interest rates and falling established home prices, households
will be much more resistant to price increases especially if dis-
cretionary household spending softens by more than expected.
That said, these headwinds may be at least partially offset by

the stronger starting point for household incomes, which has
proven more resilient than previously anticipated, and a possi-
ble bounce back in energy prices due to renewed conflict in the
Middle East.
Supply or demand driven inflation?
Inflation has steadily drifted away from the RBA’s target over
recent quarters. While the demand side is the larger driver of
inflation currently, most of the recent pick-up in TM inflation
appears to be from the supply side, and the more detailed

breakdown may understate how much.
There are several ways to split inflation into supply and de-
mand. One is to estimate how much of the rise a standard de-
mand-driven model can explain and attribute the rest to supply
(see here). Run through to Q3 2026, this approach puts most of
the recent pick-up in TM inflation down to supply. Without it,
trimmed mean would have risen from around 3.0%yr to 3.2%yr
over the year to Q3 2026, rather than the 2.9%yr to 3.7%yr

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
rise we actually saw. The supply contribution is moderate,
though, and well below its Covid-era peak. This analysis also
reveals that most of the disinflation through late-2023 and into
early-2025 reflected supply pressures unwinding, while de-
mand-driven inflation remained stickier, persisting above 3%yr.

Another is the San Francisco Fed approach, which the RBA has
also replicated (see here). The attribution is that if prices rise
and quantities fall, it is supply; if both rise, it is demand. We run
it to Q2 2026, as Q3 volume data are not yet available. Like the
first approach, it finds that demand accounts for most of infla-
tion (headline in this case). However, unlike the first approach,
it shows supply contributions easing a little.
A closer look at the categories classified as demand tells a

more nuanced story. Utilities, health and insurance all feature,
and their prices are largely shaped by government policy or are
not the categories that usually come to mind when people talk
about demand-driven inflation, and they are not very sensitive
to interest rates. You can look at electricity as a clear example
where prices in recent history have been set by government
policies (the introduction of, and then removal of, cost of living
rebates as well as policies to decarbonise the economy) or ex-

ternal supply shock (rising gas prices pushing up the marginal
cost of generation).
Categories more commonly thought of discretionary do show
up on the demand side, with recreation & culture (excluding
travel) and meals out & takeaway being the key ones. The
drawback of this sort of decomposition is that it labels cate-
gories where prices and quantities move together as demand,
even when this may reflect the ripple effects of a supply shock
elsewhere, a point our Chief Economist Luci Ellis detailed in a

note in 2024 (see In a world gone bananas, don’t squash the
other fruit). Some of this may be occurring now. In particular,
the analysis is likely not capturing the shift in spending towards
these categories resulting from travel disruptions associated
with the Middle East conflict (travel services itself was classi-
fied as a supply-side contributor in Q2). As travel normalises,
some of the “demand” contribution from recreation & culture
and meals out & takeaway should therefore fade over coming

quarters.
We would also acknowledge the work of Tim Toohey and his
analysis “The RBA has an identity problem”.

Date: 2026-11-12
